# Upstream sync audit: FigCraft fixes since the fork (Fix AI, stage 1)

This audit checks which upstream FigCraft commits since our fork point Scrim should port by hand. A merge or rebase isn't wanted: we have about 1,350 commits of our own, plus the rebrand. Nothing in Scrim's source was changed. The evidence is in [`upstream-sync/`](upstream-sync/).

**In short:**
- **Fork point and head.** We forked at `a0d1162` (18 Oct 2026). Upstream's head is now `53259cf` (31 Oct 2026), with **1,064 commits since the fork** (1,786 counting merges).
- **Licence.** Upstream is still **MIT OR Apache-2.0**. Its licence files haven't changed, and no third-party code under other terms came in. Everything below can be ported under Scrim's own terms.
- **Our tree barely moved where upstream fixed things.** For 451 bug, security, performance and dependency commits, we read the code in Scrim:
  - **300 still apply**: Scrim has the code as it was, bug included.
  - 6 are already fixed in Scrim, and 2 are unclear.
  - 143 don't apply: they fix code upstream wrote after the fork (Boards, the Vue, ArkUI and Dart targets, its own Sketch and Penpot importers, egui 0.37…).
- **Security.** Upstream ran a security audit on 22 Oct and filed 79 findings (#1054–#1131 plus #309). Most are fixed upstream, but **none of those fixes is in Scrim**. 81 of the 103 security commits apply to Scrim. Four findings were reproduced on our tree with failing tests:
  - a session member can take over another member's client id;
  - a duplicate or paste in a file with a stale id counter overwrites existing layers;
  - the ancestor check fails open on a deep chain;
  - a page guide is lost on reopen, and the next save can't be opened (data loss).
- **Dependencies.** `cargo audit` and `cargo deny` find **9 known vulnerabilities in our lock file**: rustls 0.23.45 (4), rustls-webpki 0.103.15, webpki-roots 1.0.9 and zip 2.4.2 (3). The TLS ones are fixed by a semver-compatible `cargo update` (checked with `--dry-run`). The zip ones affect `extract()`, which we never call.

## Fork point and head

| | Commit | Date | Subject |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Fork point** (`git merge-base origin/main upstream/main`; NOTICE and `docs/FORK.md` say the same) | `a0d1162976fa` | 18 Oct 2026 | Desktop: refuse unknown command-line options instead of ignoring them (#836) (#844), tag v0.7.0 |
| **Upstream head** (`storytold/figcraft` `main`, fetched 1 Nov 2026) | `53259cf50eba` | 31 Oct 2026 | ci: grant packages write permission to release workflow (v0.13.1 is the last release, `76f520f`, 30 Oct) |

- **Upstream:** https://github.com/storytold/figcraft. NOTICE, `docs/FORK.md` and the `[workspace.package] repository` key all name it.
- **What's new upstream:** 1,064 non-merge commits (475 on main's first-parent line), with 1,152 files changed, +179,087 / −44,216 lines.
- **Where it was fetched:** a throwaway clone under `~/.cache/figcraft-upstream-audit`, with `upstream` added there and fetched only. Our working copy's remotes and config were not touched. It already has an `upstream` remote, push disabled, from the fork.

## Licence

- **Never changed.**
  - `LICENSE-MIT` and `LICENSE-APACHE` are byte-identical at the fork point and at head, and Scrim's copies match them. So does `assets/app-icon/LICENSE.txt`.
  - No commit since the fork touched them.
  - The copyright line is still "Copyright (c) 2026 ArtCraft Team and the FigCraft contributors".
  - Every crate at head declares `MIT OR Apache-2.0` (most through the workspace), including the new `figcraft-vectordrawable`.
- **What did change:** four commits touched NOTICE, ATTRIBUTION.md or the agent guides:
  - `afa431b4`: the Archivo font credit, for the craft-fonts build input;
  - `236f3185`: the egui 0.37 migration added a NOTICE section for egui's icon font (MIT, John Slegers), a dependency asset;
  - `636096fa`: an Arabic catalog row;
  - `69f7e91d`: a docs tidy.

  None of this changes the licence of FigCraft's code.
- **No third-party code came in.** I looked for SPDX headers, copyright notices and new licence files in the 491 added and changed files; none came from elsewhere.
  - Upstream's `c668e217` depends on a krilla fork in `storytold/craft-libs`, which is MIT OR Apache-2.0, like the craft-docpath we already use.
  - Its `35da7b7c` and `bdb7e846` pull further craft-libs crates (craft-window, craft-ui). These are dependencies, not copied code.
- **Compatibility:** everything recommended here is available under MIT OR Apache-2.0, the same terms as Scrim. **No licence concern stops any port.**
- **Attribution still matches.** Our NOTICE keeps FigCraft's copyright line and sections, adds the fork section ("forked at storytold/figcraft commit a0d1162"), and says "based on FigCraft by the ArtCraft team". That is still correct.
- **What a port must carry:**
  - A port is a modification of FigCraft code, already covered by that section.
  - If we adopt egui 0.37 later, take upstream's NOTICE paragraph for egui-icons.ttf with it.
  - The ArtCraft marks stay out, as before: upstream's `4b12a828` refreshes the FigCraft brand colours and wordmark, and its `d17d3f48` creates a per-user "ArtCraft" install folder. Neither is ported.

## How this was done

1. **Grouping.** [`scan.py`](upstream-sync/scan.py) lists every upstream commit after the fork and sorts it into one of six groups with keyword rules.
   - I read all 1,064 subjects and corrected 170 by hand in [`overrides.tsv`](upstream-sync/overrides.tsv).
   - Every commit that closed one of upstream's audit issues counts as a security fix, whatever its subject says. They are mapped in [`audit-map.tsv`](upstream-sync/audit-map.tsv), built from each issue's closing pull requests.
2. **Scores.** For the 451 commits in groups (a) to (d), the script measures three shares:
   - **fork:** how much of the code the commit removed existed at the fork point;
   - **pre:** how much of it is still in Scrim, in the same crate;
   - **post:** how much of the fix is already here.

   Scrim had changed very little of what upstream fixed: only 4 commits scored as "changed here". About 230 had their pre-image intact, and about 110 fix code written after the fork. The scores only point at where to read.
3. **Reading.** Seven read-only reviewers each took one area: engine (three), interface (two), format and document model, and network and apps. They read each upstream diff and the matching code in Scrim, and wrote a verdict with `path:line` evidence. I took packaging, CI and the lock file. The verdicts are in [`verdicts.tsv`](upstream-sync/verdicts.tsv).
   - **still-broken:** the code path is in Scrim as it was;
   - **fixed:** Scrim already behaves correctly;
   - **n/a:** the code isn't in Scrim;
   - **unclear:** it can't be settled without running it.

   I spot-checked several claims myself: the plugin deny list, the zune-jpeg version, the `guides` key and the ping timer.
4. **Reproductions.** I wrote five tests as a separate crate outside the repository. It depends on Scrim's `figcraft-doc` and `figcraft-collab` by path, so nothing in Scrim changed, and the tests are not committed. Each test asserts the fixed behaviour; **all five fail on Scrim**. The output is in [`repro-output.txt`](upstream-sync/repro-output.txt).

   | Test | Upstream fix | What it does on Scrim |
   |---|---|---|
   | Resume someone else's client id | `10702311` (audit #1054, COL-1, High) | A second client sends a hello resuming client 1's id with the session id from any welcome. It gets id 1. |
   | Stale `next_id` | `8aa456b1` (#1064, ENG-3, High) | The document's `next_id` is set to 1 and a frame is duplicated. The copy takes `NodeId(1)`, an existing layer's id. |
   | Deep chain | `5a53425f` (#1066, COL-4, Medium) | 10,050 nested frames: `is_ancestor(top, deepest)` answers false. |
   | Page guide on reopen | `652bf35b` (#1476) | A page's guide is saved and read back, and the guide is gone. |
   | Page guide after a reopen | `652bf35b` | Add a page guide and save again: the file has two `guides` keys and doesn't open ("duplicate field `guides`"). `.scrim` files use the same serialisation (`crates/format/src/lib.rs:276`). |

5. **Issues and pull requests** were read with `gh` (signed in), read-only. Since the fork upstream has 340 issues (96 open) and 667 pull requests (533 merged). Nothing was posted, commented, opened or starred.

**Effort** for the 300 that still apply:
- **181 small (S):** under about 30 lines, and the code is the same shape;
- **93 medium (M):** our code differs, or several files change;
- **26 large (L):** depends on other upstream work, or is large.

Of the 199 bug fixes that still apply, 162 are ones a person (or an agent through MCP) would hit.

## Recommended port list

### What porting involves

- **Ports go by hand.** Upstream split its big files into folders on 26 Oct (`cmd/edit.rs` became `cmd/edit/*.rs`, `canvas.rs` became `canvas/`, and so on), so its diffs no longer apply to our layout. Read each fix and redo it in our file.
- **The rebrand adds little friction.** Crate names, directory names and most identifiers are unchanged (`figcraft-*`). What does differ:
  - user-visible strings that say FigCraft must say Scrim;
  - upstream adds strings to its own catalogs, while ours need `en` and `es` (and the grammar-safe forms for de, fi, nl and sv);
  - Scrim's own files, such as the `.scrim` format, its importers and `doc_name.rs`.
- **Frozen files are the main cost.**
  - These come up most: `crates/engine/src/lib.rs` (20 ports), `apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs` (15), `crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs` (12), `crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs` (12), `crates/doc/src/lib.rs` (12), `crates/svg/src/lib.rs` (11), `crates/format/src/fig/map.rs` (11) and `crates/text/src/layout.rs` (10).
  - Several are exactly at their cap, so a fix there must move code out first.
  - `store.rs` takes about 100 lines of server hardening, so split it before that batch.
  - `crates/ui-egui/src/layers.rs` is exactly 1,000 lines.
- **Some ports go in pairs**, because the second fixes the first. Each row says which, for example:
  - `a80df8ed` with `1a454edd`;
  - `996bf871` with `4b76c407`;
  - `8aa456b1` with `46b02d44`;
  - `0df91897` after `9426eff1` and `81f89b31`.

The priority order is: security first, then bugs users hit, then the rest. **Risk** below means the chance a port changes behaviour someone relies on, or breaks something.

### 0. Dependencies (do first; small, low risk)

| # | What | Effort | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | `cargo update -p rustls -p rustls-webpki -p webpki-roots`: 0.23.45 → 0.23.46, 0.103.15 → 0.103.16, 1.0.9 → 1.0.11. This clears RUSTSEC-2026-0346/0347/0348/0349, 0382 and 0381 for the `wss://` client in `crates/collab-net/src/tls.rs`. Verified with `--dry-run`. | S | Low: semver-compatible. 0348 needs brotli, which we don't enable. 0349 concerns post-quantum key exchange, which as far as I can tell our ring provider doesn't offer (not verified). The distrusted-intermediate fixes (0381, 0382) do apply to us. |
| 0.2 | `cargo update -p clipboard-win -p tokio`. clipboard-win 5.4.1 is yanked (upstream's `8ae2e206` refresh). tokio 1.53.2 → 1.54.1 is only pulled in by `wasm-bindgen-futures`. | S | Low |
| 0.3 | Add `cargo audit` and `cargo deny` to `cargo xtask ci`, with a committed `deny.toml`. This is upstream's open audit item #1109; upstream hasn't done it either. | S | Low. The 5 unmaintained crates (rustybuzz, ttf-parser via krilla; zvariant, zvariant_derive, zbus_names via the Linux portal) need recorded exceptions. |

### 1. Security: upstream audit findings rated High

| # | Upstream | Finding | Effort | Frozen | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | `10702311` | **COL-1:** anyone in a live session can take over another participant's client id, the host's included. **Reproduced.** | M | – | Medium: adds a per-client secret to the welcome and resume messages, a protocol change. Our desktop app, web build and server are the same code, so all ship together. |
| 1.2 | `b57c6778` | **PLG-1:** a read-only plugin can run `library.publishToServer` and upload the open document to any server it names (`crates/engine/src/plugins.rs:98` has no entry for it). | S | – | Low |
| 1.3 | `8aa456b1` + `46b02d44` | **ENG-3:** a stale `next_id` makes duplicate and paste overwrite existing layers. **Reproduced.** | S | doc `lib.rs` | Low |
| 1.4 | `b3e65c89` | **ENG-1:** a library path stored in a document is stat'ed without the local-path check. On Windows a UNC path leaks NTLM credentials (`crates/engine/src/library.rs:487`). | S | engine `library.rs` | Low |
| 1.5 | `0971d096` + `9aac391e` | A crafted progressive JPEG panics zune-jpeg 0.5.15 in the renderer and in file thumbnails. Not in the audit, but it crashes the app from a file, and the web build aborts on any panic. | M | – | Low. Updating zune-jpeg (≥ 0.5.16, upstream issue #1492) may be the smaller fix; check it first. |
| 1.6 | `a80df8ed` + `1a454edd` | **FMT-1:** single scalar fields in `.fig` (kiwi) skip the decoded-value budget. | S | – | Low; take both, or dense real files are refused. |
| 1.7 | `c857f8d0` | **FMT-2:** `Document::validate`'s issue list has no cap. | S | doc `lib.rs` | Low |
| 1.8 | `3009d19f` | **FMT-3:** `.fig` embedded image bytes are copied once per hash, not once per blob. | S | – | Low |
| 1.9 | `2baeff34` + `494387a6` | **RND-1:** the dash count isn't bounded against path length (`crates/pathops/src/lib.rs:288`). A 0.01/0.01 dash on a long line makes millions of segments. | S | – | Low; a dash too fine to draw now strokes solid. |
| 1.10 | `96eed159` | **RND-2:** nested isolated layers each allocate a full-view pixmap, with no total. | M | – | Medium (render path) |
| 1.11 | `4da6a11b` | **ENG-2:** `world_render_bounds` recursion has no depth limit (`crates/doc/src/lib.rs:689`). | S | doc `lib.rs` | Low |
| 1.12 | `08173cc5` | **COL-2:** fixes for a refused batch are quadratic. | S | – | Low |

### 2. Security: Medium

Also High in effect:
- `f1c2ee81` (COL-9) lets a collaborator put other bytes under a content hash, or name an image `../../x`, which a save writes as a zip member. It also needs `57cf1bec`, `7cce115a` and `949d74f7`.
- `ef4c0aac` lets an agent confined to automation roots still publish, host, join or browse.

| # | Upstream | Finding | Effort | Frozen |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | `f1c2ee81` (+ its three follow-ups) | COL-9: image entries in live sessions aren't checked against their hash or size. | M | doc `lib.rs` |
| 2.2 | `d5de4be1`, `5d7eb04d`, `4f39406f`, `c80d1e7a` | COL-6, COL-10, COL-8: pre-hello slowloris; full-size messages before the token check; kicked connections that keep their queue. | S–M | `hub.rs` |
| 2.3 | `5a53425f` | COL-4: `is_ancestor` fails open past 10,000 steps. **Reproduced.** | S | doc `lib.rs` |
| 2.4 | `0d9c2c3f` | ENG-4: components that contain each other through another component aren't refused, and their instances expand exponentially. | L | `components.rs`, `cmd/component.rs` |
| 2.5 | `9573b8b2`, `62c69ba7`, `cc0976a4` | ENG-5: a panic inside a grouped command leaves the document half edited and live documents held. | M | engine `lib.rs` |
| 2.6 | `ef4c0aac` (+ `e6059b35`) | IPC-3: commands confined to automation roots can still reach the network. | M | `live.rs` |
| 2.7 | `7b886404` | IPC-5: opening a file contacts every team library server it names, with usage counts, as soon as Assets shows. | M | engine `lib.rs`, `assets_panel.rs` |
| 2.8 | `e2742795`, `fcde83a5` + `58d791c8`, `675777e0`, `d5ce10f0` | FMT-7, FMT-4 (in part), FMT-5, FMT-6: unpack and decompression budgets, and quadratic `.fig` instance expansion. | M | `fig/map.rs`, doc `lib.rs` |
| 2.9 | `35f5faa2`, `2e3d9b6f`, `7fdbda9f`, `393abf80` | RND-4, RND-3, RND-5, RND-6: SVG nesting depth after DTD expansion; pattern tiles re-rendered per use; one depth cap and enough stack for render threads; grid auto-placement. | S–M | `svg/lib.rs`, `layout/lib.rs` |
| 2.10 | `d7d6bc84`, `436cfa1b`, `f6ccef0c`, `fb0465f3`, `2d02f289` | SRV-1, COL-7, SRV-3, SRV-2, COL-5: per-address storage caps per IPv6 prefix; a bounded replay log; an admin request budget and join codes found by hash; idle join/leave I/O; out-of-range ids blocking joins. | M–L | `store.rs`, `hub.rs` |
| 2.11 | `53f47706` | IPC-4: prototype links open with no click and no notice (delay or hover triggers). Needs upstream's link notice (`ae57bf84`, a feature) first. | L | `present.rs` |

### 3. Security: Low, and hardening

These are mostly small, and they cluster, so batch them by file:
- **The collaboration server** (`apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs`, frozen; split it first): `8da99e53` (operator key compared by hash), `11cf6fb1`, `49c0d4c7`, `37500198`, `eafa1a1d`, `51a4509c`, `8c46b733`, `82779ec0`, `21d66646`, `8ab0de26`, `7d18fa39`.
- **Engine and doc:**
  - `9c0d1c19`: a refused command still overwrites the clipboard;
  - `7bc59463`: `layout.setTrack` track count is unclamped;
  - `24cf6432`: a batch keeps one undo snapshot per step;
  - `8864e42d`: plugin settings survive a re-registered plugin id;
  - `98298a35`: image member names in archives aren't validated;
  - `b2885452`: plugins and agents see other documents' paths in `doc.list`;
  - `c4717749`: files read by path aren't capped or checked as regular files;
  - `f1eaf3fc`: client-side maps are unbounded;
  - `cc3052de`: boolean operations have no input cap (L);
  - `974ab395`: the web build has no panic guard (L).
- **Text and SVG:**
  - `cb38f68c`, `3739b286`, `0cca5698`: COLR layers, PNG glyph sizes, decoration steps and rename expansions (S, but `0cca5698` touches frozen `text/layout.rs`);
  - `9f788753`: linear vector import and validation;
  - `b5e5c210`: SVG `unique_id`;
  - `bdb6a059`: tests.
- **The window's control channel:** `43a255fe` (no depth clamp; MCP's headless path already has one), `c6dba8c8`, `67ae35b1`, `7e114c34`, `57e08729`, `8ae76d9b`.
- **The CLI:** `c3cb8c3e` (confine info, export, convert, render and run to automation roots), `d114c6ed`.
- **Packaging and CI:**
  - `996bf871` + `4b76c407`: symlinks in `xtask assets`, the signing temp folder, icon writes;
  - `cb796655`: `curl --proto '=https'`;
  - `c4810235`: pin appimagetool and the Windows signing client by SHA-256;
  - `a86897dc` and `40d7ce14`: actions pinned by commit, checkout v7;
  - `c668e217`: krilla with a decode cap. It adds a craft-libs `[patch]`, and our renderer already bounds the image first.

### 5. Performance

Every performance item still applies:
- **Large selections and drags** (`5247f73f` → `db725dc7` → `99440fa9`, all L; `38a4fecc`, `27920c5e`, `646ac60b`): overlays and the Design panel rescan the whole selection every frame, and drags re-normalise every layer.
- **Rendering:** `a3d70d4c` (an edit re-renders only where the changed layer paints), `e32f8f2e` (large images draw from a smaller copy), `adb1b430` (faster blur and spread in raster export; our `fx.rs` matches upstream's code before it), `e37e0bc2` (cache glass shapes), `6acbd913` (load only the fallback fonts drawn with).
- **The server:** `0f85c229`.
- **The browser build:** `6d8b0e68`, `f24a8312`.
- **Cold zooms:** `79d436a4` (a preview for cold zooms and page switches).

Measure before and after each port; most are M or L.

### Not recommended

- The egui 0.37 migration (`236f3185`) and its follow-ups: a separate decision.
- craft-libs crates we don't use (craft-window `35da7b7c`, craft-ui, craft-update, craft-font-catalog `e04c5d22`): each adds a git dependency on FigCraft's infrastructure.
- The 251 features, which are listed but not proposed.

## Open upstream issues that apply to shared code

These are still open upstream, so there's no fix to port. The code they point at is still in Scrim, checked at the locations the issues cite:

| Issue | Finding | In Scrim |
|---|---|---|
| #1072 IPC-1 (Medium) | The control channel has no authentication: any local process can run any engine command and write any path. An unmerged branch, `upstream/harden/control-channel-token`, adds a token. | Yes, `apps/figcraft/src/control_server.rs`. It is started only with `--control` or `FIGCRAFT_CONTROL_PORT`, which lowers the exposure; our live checks use it. |
| #1110 / #1111 (Low) | The control channel: 8 slots with a 60 s idle timeout can be held by one peer; 32 MiB lines are parsed whole. | Yes, the same constants (`control_server.rs:19-22`) |
| #1131 (Low) | Live sessions hosted from the desktop use plain `ws://` on the LAN, and the session token is sent in clear. | Yes (`crates/collab-net/src/host.rs:72`, `invite.rs:46`) |
| #1076 FMT-4 (Medium) | `.fig` zip images have no total budget. Partly fixed by `fcde83a5`. | Yes (`crates/format/src/fig/mod.rs:63`) |
| #1094 IPC-2 (Low-Medium) | Automation roots canonicalize, and so contact, a UNC path before refusing it; a time-of-check window. | Yes (`crates/engine/src/file_access.rs`) |
| #1103 RND-8 (Low) | Morphology costs O(w·h·r) with r up to 400. | Yes (`crates/render/src/fx.rs:110`) |
| #1105 RND-10 (Low) | Full RGBA buffers held until the PDF is written. | Yes (`crates/pdf/src/draw.rs`) |
| #1107, #1108, #1109 | zip extract advisories, unmaintained crates, no `deny.toml`, no `overflow-checks` in release. | Yes, see [Dependencies](#dependencies-and-advisories) |

Upstream's open bug reports are user reports; I didn't verify them on Scrim. These are in code we share and may apply:
- #1745: pen tool undo;
- #1748: page background opacity can't be changed;
- #1761: Ctrl with continuous selection;
- #1572: shape builder artifacts;
- #1767 and #1768: scaled elements and complex subtraction in `.fig` import;
- #1422: clicks register below the cursor;
- #1388: one file opened in two windows;
- #1543: Windows startup fails creating the egui pipeline;
- #1538: the macOS canvas isn't colour-managed on wide-gamut displays;
- #963: newly installed fonts aren't detected on Linux.

The rest are about upstream's own features or builds.

## Dependencies and advisories

The output is in [`advisories.txt`](upstream-sync/advisories.txt). `cargo-audit` 0.22.2 and `cargo-deny` 0.20.2 were built into `~/.cache/scrim-target/tools` (the shared target folder) and run against Scrim's `Cargo.lock` at `67e0e41`: 599 packages, and 1,395 advisories in the RustSec database fetched on 1 Nov 2026. Both tools agree.

| Crate (ours) | Advisory | Reachable in Scrim? | Fix | Upstream |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rustls 0.23.45 | RUSTSEC-2026-0346 (`Debug` exposes TLS secrets), 0347 (ECH), 0348 (brotli certificate decompression), 0349 (post-quantum downgrade) | 0346 only if a session value is debug-printed; 0347 concerns ECH, which we don't configure; 0349 probably doesn't apply with the ring provider (not verified); 0348 needs the `brotli` feature, which is off (features: std, ring, tls12, logging) | 0.23.46, semver-compatible | Moved to 0.23.46 in `35da7b7c` |
| rustls-webpki 0.103.15 | RUSTSEC-2026-0382: may accept distrusted intermediates | **Yes**, when verifying a `wss://` server | 0.103.16 | 0.103.16 |
| webpki-roots 1.0.9 | RUSTSEC-2026-0381: distrusted emSign intermediates | **Yes** | 1.0.11 | 1.0.11 |
| zip 2.4.2 | RUSTSEC-2026-0367/0368/0369: `extract()` keeps SUID bits and follows symlinks | **No**: nothing in Scrim calls `extract` or `ZipStreamReader` (we read members ourselves) | ≥ 9.0, a major bump | Still on 2.6.1 in the crates we share (zip 9.1.1 only for craft-update); upstream's audit #1107 calls it not reachable |
| tokio 1.53.2 | RUSTSEC-2026-0386 (unsound `AsyncFd::new`) | No: tokio comes only through `wasm-bindgen-futures`, and we never use `AsyncFd` | No patched version listed; 1.54.1 is available | 1.54.1 |
| clipboard-win 5.4.1 | yanked | – | 5.4.3 | 5.4.3 (`8ae2e206`) |
| rustybuzz 0.20.1, ttf-parser 0.25.1 (through krilla, PDF); zbus_names, zvariant, zvariant_derive (through the Linux portal) | unmaintained | – | none yet | same versions |

Upstream's dependency commits: 20 in group (d), of which 5 apply here:
- `8ae2e206`: refresh;
- `c668e217`: krilla through craft-libs;
- `40d7ce14`: actions/checkout;
- `35da7b7c`: craft-window (not recommended);
- `e04c5d22`: craft-font-catalog (not recommended).

The rest go with egui 0.37, or with craft-libs crates we don't have.

## What could not be determined

- **Behaviour against real upstream files and builds.** The verdicts come from reading code. Five were reproduced with tests; the rest were not run. A few rest on a reading that a test would settle; each row says so:
  - `0df91897`: effects drawn at zoom × page size;
  - `fe787270`: kurbo dropping zero-length dashes;
  - `7fdbda9f`: stack depth, inferred from upstream's measurements;
  - `6394e960`: var modes kept only on the instance root.
- **Two unclear rows,** `71323ee7` and `0daecb70`: field text moving on focus. Upstream blames egui 0.37's minimum text field height, which egui 0.36 may not have.
- **Platform-specific items checked by reading only:**
  - `c6dd115e`: text copy on GNOME Wayland;
  - `fbb4a22e`: live sessions over 64 MiB, which tungstenite 0.30 may not enforce;
  - `05f81110`: the graphics fallback at startup;
  - the Windows UNC credential leak (`b3e65c89`).
- **The keyword grouping is a judgement.** All 1,064 subjects were read and corrected, but the split between "bug fix" and "feature" is debatable for commits like `87e42a14` (dash phase) or `b8236920` (duplicating a main component makes an instance). Groups (e) and (f) weren't checked against our tree.
- **The scores** only measure how much text matches. A fix that moved code can score as post-fork, so every row was read rather than taken from its score.
- **Upstream issues and pull requests:** the open bug reports above weren't reproduced on Scrim.

Noticed while reading, outside this audit's scope:
- `packaging/server/install.sh:30` still downloads releases from `storytold/figcraft`.
- Our Flatpak manifests grant no network, so live sessions and Share > Online can't connect from the Flatpak.
- Our own Sketch, Penpot and FREE importers cap total bytes, but not the compression ratio.
- Scrim's MCP HTTP endpoint (`crates/mcp/src/http.rs:306`) retries a failed accept at once, the bug `42502a81` fixes in the control server.

## Applicability table

One row per bug, security, performance and dependency commit: security first, then bugs, performance and dependencies, each with "still broken" first.
- **Port:** S, M or L.
- **Frozen files:** the files over 1,000 lines the port would touch.
- **Users hit it:** "yes" if a person or an MCP agent would hit it; "no" if only hostile input or internal hardening reaches it.

The machine-readable form is [`verdicts.tsv`](upstream-sync/verdicts.tsv).

| Commit | Group | Subject | Verdict | Port | Frozen files | Users hit it | Evidence (Scrim's code) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bdb6a059` | b | M8: regression tests for one-sided strokes on rounded shapes and XML-forbidden characters in SVG (#1351, #1124) (#1719)(upstream audit #1124 (Minor 15, Low)) | applies, still broken | S | - | no | Regression tests only (audit #1124): one-sided strokes on rounded shapes and XML-forbidden characters in SVG export. Their fixes, 6d748536 and 31d4c6eb, are still-broken in Scrim (crates/svg), so these tests would fail here. Reproduction: port with 6d748536 and 31d4c6eb |
| `ef4c0aac` | b | M8: commands confined to automation roots reach no server (#1095)(upstream audit #1095 (IPC-3, Low)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/live.rs | no | With automation roots active (crates/engine/src/file_access.rs:181 confined, used by the MCP server at crates/mcp/src/server.rs:254), nothing stops a command reaching the network: Lives::start (crates/engine/src/live.rs:727), Session::net (crates/engine/src/background.rs:129-130) and refresh_server_libraries (crates/engine/src/library_server.rs:176) all only check that a link exists. Upstream adds file_access::network_refused and checks it in each. Reproduction: Run collab.publish {server: ...}, collab.host or plugin.directory.list inside file_access::confined(Some(roots)): the request reaches the link, where it should be refused before the link is asked. |
| `2e9212d9` | b | M8: text style dedupe is O(spans) (#1102)(upstream audit #1102 (RND-7, Low-Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/text/src/layout.rs | no | crates/text/src/layout.rs:315-323 layout() still dedupes spans into styles with styles.iter().position, a linear scan per span, so a text with many distinct runs costs O(spans x styles) full TextStyle comparisons on every layout. layout.rs is frozen at 1323 lines, so the fingerprint buckets have to live in a new module. Reproduction: A text of 20,000 characters where every character has its own font size (20,000 distinct styles): layout() does about 200 million TextStyle comparisons; timing a bench before and after shows the quadratic growth. |
| `c4717749` | b | M8: every file a command reads by path is a regular file within a cap (#1114) (#1625)(upstream audit #1114 (Minor 5, Low)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/colorpicker.rs | no | file_access::read (crates/engine/src/file_access.rs:232) reads any path with no type or size check, and file.open (crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs:435), image.place (crates/engine/src/cmd/create.rs:377), the image fill picker (crates/ui-egui/src/colorpicker.rs:518) and the CLI's run_script use it; read_capped (:238) also reads without a bound after checking. Our own claude_desktop.rs:379 and agent_clients.rs:539 read the same way. Reproduction: image.place {path: "/dev/zero"} or file.open on a FIFO hangs or fills memory, where it should be refused as not a regular file. |
| `7b886404` | b | M8: Opening a file contacts no team library server it names until you check it (#1621)(upstream audit #1097 (IPC-5, Low)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/lib.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/assets_panel.rs | yes | Session::refresh_server_libraries (crates/engine/src/library_server.rs:161-178) sends a Fetch, with the document's usage counts, to every server link in the open file's libraries.enabled. The Assets panel calls it as soon as it shows (assets_panel.rs:289-291). Nothing (no team_libraries allow-list, no library.check) limits this to libraries the user added. Reproduction: Open a file whose libraries.enabled names wss://attacker.example/lib with the Assets tab showing: a background library request goes to that host without the user ever adding it. |
| `f1eaf3fc` | b | M8: cap the people a client keeps track of and the deleted ids a session remembers (#1622)(upstream audit #1121 (Minor 12, Low)) | applies, still broken | L | crates/engine/src/live.rs | no | There is no MAX_PEERS or MAX_DELETED anywhere: the collab server's deleted set only grows (crates/collab/src/server.rs:57, extended at :452), and the client keeps every peer a server announces (crates/collab-net/src/join.rs:191 clones the whole welcome list, :221 grows seen.peers). Reproduction: A hostile server sends a welcome listing 1,000,000 peers, or keeps sending peer-joined events: the client stores them all; a session that deletes 10^6 layers keeps 10^6 ids. |
| `b2885452` | b | M8: doc.list gives AI agents and plugins file names, not paths (#1117) (#1620)(upstream audit #1117 (Minor 8, Low)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs | no | doc.list (crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs:240-248) returns each tab's full `path` to every caller, and plugins are explicitly allowed to call it (ALLOWED at crates/engine/src/plugins.rs:120). Upstream strips `path` for agents and plugins. Ours has no Session::agent flag, so the agent half needs our own way to tell an MCP call. Reproduction: A plugin calling doc.list gets ["/Users/<name>/Documents/x.figcraft"]; it should get only names. |
| `7d18fa39` | b | M8: libraries and plugins larger than the download burst can be downloaded, more slowly (#1089) (#1619)(upstream audit #1089 (SRV-5, Low)) | applies, still broken | S | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs | yes | apps/figcraft-server/src/abuse.rs:160 and :205 charge downloads with Bucket::take_n, which never holds more than DOWNLOAD_BURST (16 MiB, abuse.rs:31), and store.rs:938 does the same for the global budget; there is no take_spread in crates/collab-net/src/net.rs:267. The store.rs change is an in-place swap. Reproduction: Publish a team library whose archive is 20 MiB, then request it from a fresh address: always BUSY_DOWNLOADS ("too many downloads…"), however long you wait. |
| `24cf6432` | b | M8: a batch keeps one undo snapshot, not one per step (#1099) (#1610)(upstream audit #1099 (ENG-7, Low)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/lib.rs | no | Session::grouped (crates/engine/src/lib.rs:1164-1214) lifts the history limit and lets every command inside push its own snapshot through push_undo (:476), truncating them only when the group ends. Upstream's group_step makes later edits in a group reuse the step it already pushed. Reproduction: In a grouped closure running 1,000 node.set renames, history.undo.len() reaches base+1000 during the group, where it should stay at base+1. |
| `cc0976a4` | b | #1609: a grouped panic keeps its payload and lets later live edits sync(upstream audit #1085 (ENG-5, Medium)) | applies, still broken | S | - | no | Test-only commit; the behaviour it pins is broken in ours because Session::grouped (crates/engine/src/lib.rs:1164) has no catch_unwind (see 9573b8b2), so a panic skips the live_hold(false) calls (:1177-1179) and the original payload is never re-raised by a rollback. Reproduction: In a hosted live session, grouped(\|s\| { node.set; panic!() }) under catch_unwind leaves the document edited and held, and a later node.set never goes out. |
| `4224f4b6` | b | M8: deep files import on the browser's 1 MB stack (#1101) (#1600)(upstream audit #1101 (FMT-8, Low-Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/format/src/rest/mod.rs | no | Ctx::build (crates/format/src/rest/mod.rs:485) still recurses once per level (:541) to MAX_DEPTH 256, about 4 KB a level, and the .fig import's second stage runs through it; apps/figcraft-web runs on a 1 MB wasm stack. Native main threads (8 MB) are fine. Reproduction: In a 1 MB-stack thread (std::thread::Builder::stack_size(1<<20)), import a REST JSON or .fig with 256 nested frames: it overflows the stack instead of importing. |
| `fcde83a5` | b | M8: .fig ZIP image reads share a total byte budget (part of #1076) | applies, still broken | M | - | no | Archive::image (crates/format/src/fig/mod.rs:222-229) reads each referenced images/<hash> member up to MAX_IMAGE (256 MiB) with no total across the import, and MAX_FILE caps only the packed size. Scrim's own Sketch, Penpot and FREE importers do keep a 1 GiB total (sketch/mod.rs:169). Reproduction: A .fig zip with 30 images/<hash> members, each 256 MiB of zeros deflated (about 250 KB each), referenced by 30 image fills: the import allocates about 7.5 GB instead of skipping the images past a budget with a warning. |
| `8da99e53` | b | M9: the server compares the operator key by its hash (#1130)(upstream audit #1130 (Minor 21, Low)) | applies, still broken | S | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs | no | store.rs:1416 manage() calls same_secret(key, &self.key) on the raw key, and crates/collab/src/lib.rs:50 returns early when the lengths differ, so timing reveals the key's length. Upstream compares SHA-256 digests of both. Reproduction: Time manage() with keys of lengths 1..64: the length equal to the real key's takes measurably longer (the only one that runs the byte loop). |
| `7fdbda9f` | b | M8: One depth cap for a whole render, and render threads with room for it (#1605)(upstream audit #1082 (RND-5, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/svg/src/lib.rs | no | crates/render/src/pattern.rs:66 cell_pixmap() still draws a pattern source with draw_node(..., 0), restarting the 256-level MAX_EXPORT_DEPTH cap (draw.rs:469), so patterns inside pattern sources can stack several full-depth renders; nothing in crates/render sets a thread-local layer depth or a bigger thread stack. The canvas render thread (crates/ui-egui/src/render_worker.rs:202) and rayon's band threads keep the default 2 MiB stack. Reproduction: A file with a 250-deep layer chain used as a pattern source, whose innermost layer has a pattern fill of another 250-deep chain: rendering recurses past 256 levels and overflows a 2 MiB thread stack (crash); expected the depth cap to bound the whole render. |
| `fb0465f3` | b | M8: a server file everyone left stays loaded a minute and its open time is written hourly, so idle join/leave does no disk I/O (#1074) (#1606)(upstream audit #1074 (SRV-2, Medium)) | applies, still broken | L | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs | no | store.rs:707 touch() rewrites meta.json (an atomic, fsynced write) at every join and leave, and idle() (:1387) drops the session, so a rejoin re-reads and re-decodes the snapshot. There is no Parked/TOUCH_EVERY; about 150 lines into a frozen file, so it needs a split first. Reproduction: Count file writes while one client joins and leaves an idle file ten times: 10+ meta.json writes and 10 snapshot reads (upstream: none after the first). |
| `9573b8b2` | b | M8: a grouped command whose closure panics leaves the document as before (#1085)(upstream audit #1085 (ENG-5, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/lib.rs | no | Session::grouped (crates/engine/src/lib.rs:1164-1214) calls f(self) bare, so a panic in the closure outside a command skips the rollback: documents stay half edited, history.limit stays usize::MAX and live documents stay held. The MCP server catches the unwind (crates/mcp/src/server.rs:254) and carries on with that state. Reproduction: grouped("G", \|s\| { s.execute("node.set", rename)?; panic!() }) under catch_unwind: the rename stays and history.limit is usize::MAX. |
| `e2742795` | b | M8: one unpack budget per .figcraft open (#1079) (#1599)(upstream audit #1079 (FMT-7, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | - | no | load_within (crates/format/src/lib.rs:499-531) gives the document, images (4 GiB), library (u64::MAX), versions (1 GiB) and branches separate caps with no total per open, so one crafted file can make the app unpack about 10 GB. Reproduction: A .figcraft with a 4 GiB set of highly compressible images plus a 1 GiB version history and branches: load_bytes unpacks all of it; it should stop at one total (upstream 6 GiB) and report the rest. |
| `675777e0` | b | M8: .fig instance expansion without quadratic walks and copies (#1077) (#1594)(upstream audit #1077 (FMT-5, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/format/src/fig/map.rs | no | Mapper's instance expansion still copies every outer override row (map.rs:1975 iterates inherited.overrides) and clones the outer props and slot_contents maps per nested instance (map.rs:2027-2028), and VARIABLE nodes still join the layer tree (map.rs:423). That is quadratic on crafted nesting (upstream measured 13.8 s down to 21 ms). Reproduction: A .fig with a 200-deep chain of nested instances, each carrying 1,000 override rows: mapping time grows with depth times rows (seconds) instead of staying linear. |
| `393abf80` | b | M8: Grid auto-placement on an occupancy bitmap (#1593)(upstream audit #1083 (RND-6, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | crates/layout/src/lib.rs | no | grid_place (crates/layout/src/lib.rs:648-) still tests every cell of a span at every candidate with Vec<Vec<bool>> free and mark closures. A 1000 x 1000 grid takes about 192 ms per layout upstream; placements would be unchanged by the port. Reproduction: A grid auto layout with 1000 columns and 1000 one-cell children, the first anchored at the last row: grid_place takes about 200 ms per call instead of under 1 ms. |
| `974ab395` | b | M8: keep unsaved files when a panic stops the browser build (#1123) (#1580)(upstream audit #1123 (Minor 14, Low)) | applies, still broken | L | crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs | yes | apps/figcraft-web/src/web.rs has no panic hook and there is no apps/figcraft-web/src/recovery.rs; crates/engine/src/guard.rs:7 relies on catch_unwind, which does nothing on wasm32, so a panic ends the page with its unsaved changes. Upstream's fix is about 300 lines: local-storage copies, file.recover taking base64, and moving the recovery helpers into the engine. Reproduction: In the web build, edit a file, then trigger any panic (e.g. a debug command that panics): the page stops, and reloading shows nothing to recover. |
| `cc3052de` | b | M8: cap the input of boolean operations (#1104) (#1581)(upstream audit #1104 (RND-9, Low)) | applies, still broken | L | crates/engine/src/lib.rs | no | crates/pathops/src/lib.rs:69-110 binary/boolean_step/boolean_ops hand any input straight to linesweeper; PathOpsError has only BadInput (line 25) and there is no budget module, so a boolean group with hundreds of thousands of crossing segments runs for unbounded time and memory on every render. The port is upstream's new budget.rs (segment and crossing counts), refusal caching in crates/render/src/boolean.rs and a vector.flatten refusal in crates/engine/src/cmd/structure.rs. Reproduction: A file with a boolean union of two vectors each holding 200,000 zig-zag segments that cross each other: opening it hangs the canvas render (minutes, gigabytes); expected the group to draw without its result and vector.flatten to fail with TooComplex. |
| `f6ccef0c` | b | M9: a per-address budget for server admin requests, and join codes found by hash (#1075) (#1552)(upstream audit #1075 (SRV-3, Medium)) | applies, still broken | M | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs, crates/collab-net/src/hub.rs | no | store.rs:1494 admin() has no per-address budget (abuse.rs has no Action::Admin), so a holder of an owner token can make unlimited file-changing requests. store.rs:825 find_code also scans every file's links on every typed code instead of an index keyed by code hash. The fix changes the hub's admin() signature (hub.rs:158/1523) and adds an index to store.rs. Reproduction: With an owner token, send 1,000 Admin rename requests in a minute: all succeed, each writing meta.json (upstream refuses after 60 with BUSY_ADMIN). |
| `2e3d9b6f` | b | M8: SVG pattern tiles drawn once per scale, within the layer budget (#1080) (#1551)(upstream audit #1080 (RND-3, Medium)) | applies, still broken | S | - | yes | crates/svg/src/import/paint.rs:233-263 render_tile() imports, renders and PNG-encodes a pattern's tile again for every use, with no cache by pattern and scale, and its sub-import starts a fresh Ctx whose layer count is not carried back, so pattern contents escape the import's layer budget (cx.full()). Reproduction: An SVG with one <pattern> of 1,000 small shapes used as the fill of 2,000 rectangles: the import builds and renders the tile 2,000 times (2 million layers outside the budget); expected one tile per pattern and scale, counted in the budget. |
| `96eed159` | b | M8: mask groups draw over their own area, nested layers share one memory budget (#1061) (#1535)(upstream audit #1061 (RND-2, High)) | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | crates/render/src/draw.rs:786-788 draw_masked() still allocates its mask, content and temporary pixmaps at the full size of the target pixmap whatever the mask covers, and nothing budgets the isolated layers nested renders hold at once (no layer_budget module). Our backdrop.rs has no replace() helper, so that piece comes with the port. Reproduction: A 40x40 mask group exported at 3000x2000: draw_masked allocates about 96 MB of pixmaps instead of about 60 kB; ten nested mask groups on a large export multiply that until allocation fails. |
| `0d9c2c3f` | b | M3: components that would contain themselves are refused, and loops in files are repaired (#1084) (#1540)(upstream audit #1084 (ENG-4, Medium)) | applies, still broken | L | crates/engine/src/components.rs, crates/engine/src/cmd/component.rs, crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs, crates/engine/src/lib.rs, crates/engine/src/live.rs | yes | Only direct self-containment is refused (create_instance_at at crates/engine/src/components.rs:268, instance.place at crates/engine/src/cmd/component.rs:274), and sync_instance_depth stops only when an instance is inside its own main (components.rs:565) or past MAX_DEPTH 16 (:40). Indirect loops are accepted, and files holding them aren't repaired on open. Reproduction: Component A, component B containing an instance of A, then component.instance of B with parent A: it is accepted, and an instance of A on the page expands A>B>A… to 16 levels (exponentially with two nested instances per level). |
| `436cfa1b` | b | M9: the replay log is bounded in bytes by default (#1069) (#1524)(upstream audit #1069 (COL-7, Medium)) | applies, still broken | S | - | no | crates/collab/src/lib.rs:105 still defaults max_log_bytes to usize::MAX, and apps/figcraft-server/src/config.rs:287 bounds it only on open servers, so a desktop host or a private server keeps the replay log bounded only by count. There is no max_log_mb setting. Reproduction: On a private server, apply nine batches that each rename to an 8 MiB name: the log keeps all ~75 MB in memory (upstream trims to 64 MiB). |

Reproduction: A progressive JPEG (SOF2) whose second component has a sampling factor of 2x2 while the first has 1x1, used as an image fill or as a .scrim thumbnail: decode panics in zune-jpeg's upsampler instead of returning None. |
| `9aac391e` | b | M8: a progressive JPEG sampling a component finer than its first is refused, not decoded | applies, still broken | M | - | no | Our Cargo.lock still pins zune-jpeg 0.5.15 (the decoder with the upsampler assert, zune-image#236) and crates/render/src/images.rs:100 decode_within() hands any JPEG to image without a frame-header check (no jpeg_finer_than_first). | `71323ee7` | a | M8: search fields in the left panels keep their text in the middle (#1703) (#1709) | unclear | M | crates/ui-egui/src/widgets.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/assets_panel.rs | yes | Our Layers search (crates/ui-egui/src/layers_header.rs:81-105) and Assets search (assets_panel.rs:299-318) still put the TextEdit in a 14 pt box, as upstream's pre-fix code did. But upstream blames egui 0.37's theme minimum editor height, and egui 0.36.2's TextEdit sizes a frameless editor to its line height only (egui builder.rs:715), so the text may already sit centred here. But upstream's cause is egui 0.37's 24 pt minimum TextEdit height, which egui 0.36.2 doesn't impose (builder.rs:715), so the text may not jump here. Reproduction: Settle it with a headless test: focus a sidebar number field and compare the y of the focused editor's galley with the value painted while idle. |
| `646ac60b` | c | M8: an edit checks only the layers it changed for out-of-range positions and sizes (#1672) | applies, still broken | S | crates/engine/src/lib.rs | yes | check_finite_geometry at crates/engine/src/lib.rs:911-923 still scans ds.doc.nodes() for every layer after every command; upstream walks Document::changed_ids (which ours has at crates/doc/src/lib.rs:351) instead. Reproduction: In a 200,000-layer document, 100 renames take about 0.6 s, against about 0.02 s with the diff (upstream's measurement). |
| `db725dc7` | c | M8: the Design panel and the selection overlays stop going over a large selection every frame (#1571) (#1660) | applies, still broken | L | crates/ui-egui/src/right.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs | yes | The Design panel still scans the whole selection with doc.node lookups many times a frame (e.g. crates/ui-egui/src/right.rs:781 radius_ok, and the same all()/any() checks in layout_panel.rs). We have no selection_cache module. The port needs 5247f73f's cache first. Reproduction: Select 648 layers and measure the UI pass per idle frame: each sel.iter().all(...) check repeats every frame, which upstream measured at about 2.5 ms. |
| `27920c5e` | c | M8: a drag moving many layers normalises only what sits around them, not each layer's bindings, sizing, text and instance checks (#1571) (#1656) | applies, still broken | M | crates/engine/src/lib.rs | yes | Our normalize (crates/engine/src/lib.rs:1253 onward) puts every touched layer through bindings, style links, coherent sizing, text auto-size and instance sync, with no only_moved_layers shortcut for layers whose transform alone changed. Reproduction: Drag 500 plain rectangles: each preview frame re-runs binding_scope, coherent_sizing and sync_after_edit for all 500, where upstream does only the parents and groups around them. |
| `6acbd913` | c | M8: text reads only the fallback fonts it draws with | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | crates/text/src/fontdb.rs:653 fallbacks_for() still calls find() for each of the 20 FALLBACK_FAMILIES, and find() (line 595) loads every pending face of the family, with file bytes shared only within one family (load_family, line 575). Our FontDb has no generation counter, so upstream's per-style fallback cache (FallbackLists) and cross-family file sharing need reworking rather than a straight copy. Reproduction: On Linux with Noto Sans CJK installed, lay out one Latin text: all CJK collection files (about 7 files, over 100 MB) are read into memory, the SC and KR faces twice; expected only the nearest face per family. |
| `99440fa9` | c | M8: a drag that only moves the selection shifts what was worked out at its start (#1571) (#1640) | applies, still broken | L | crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/right.rs, crates/engine/src/lib.rs, crates/engine/src/tools.rs | yes | crates/engine/src has no Interaction::moved_by. During a move drag, canvas.rs:1018-1050 rebuilds every selected outline, selection_box and selection_frame from the live document each frame. This depends on 5247f73f and db725dc7. Reproduction: Drag 648 selected layers and profile a frame: outlines, slots and Design panel values are recomputed on every move instead of shifted. |
| `a3d70d4c` | c | M8: an edit renders where the changed layer paints, its effects' margin once | applies, still broken | S | - | yes | crates/render/src/draw.rs:423-427 paint_bounds() still inflates the paint bounds (which already take in effects via paint_reach) by effect_margin again, and the damage rect is then rendered with culling that adds that margin a third time, so an edit near shadows or blurs patches about twice the pixels it can change and falls back to full renders sooner (retained.rs MAX_PARTIAL_SHARE). Reproduction: Move a 120x120 rectangle with a drop shadow offset 40 in the retained renderer: patch() reports about 122,800 pixels rendered for about 56,900 that can change; expected the smaller count (a perf assertion on the returned pixel count). |
| `5247f73f` | c | M8: a large selection's overlay and Design panel values are kept for frames where nothing changed (#1611) | applies, still broken | L | crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/right.rs, crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs | yes | The selection overlay (crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs:1019-1050) redraws every selected layer's outline and recomputes selection_box, selection_frame and locked_in each frame, and slot_badges (:752) walks the selection again. Nothing keeps these per document snapshot. Reproduction: Select 648 layers and leave the pointer moving over the canvas: overlay time stays about 2 ms a frame (upstream measured 1.99 ms, 0.86 ms after the fix). |
| `38a4fecc` | c | M8: hover hit-tests once per pointer move, and a moved auto layout frame keeps its layout (#1571) (#1601) | applies, still broken | M | crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs, crates/engine/src/lib.rs | yes | The canvas sends a Hover to the tools on every frame the pointer is over it (crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs:626-649), with no HoverKey check for an unchanged state. The engine re-lays out a frame whose only change is its transform: affects_layout (engine/src/lib.rs:1382) counts the transform, and the chain (:1343-1345) includes the frame itself. Reproduction: Drag an auto layout frame with 600 children: each move re-runs that frame's own layout. Hovering with no change hit-tests about three times per pointer move. |
| `6d8b0e68` | c | M8: the browser draws a zoom's full render a band at a time | applies, still broken | L | - | yes | In the browser build our Inline worker (crates/ui-egui/src/render_worker.rs:268-300, poll) draws the refined full render with renders.refine(&job) in one frame, and crates/render/src/retained.rs has no stepwise Begun/Steps API, so on a heavy page no input is handled for the seconds that render takes. The port is the band-at-a-time Retained::begin/finish plus refine_for with a frame time budget. Reproduction: In the WebAssembly build, on a page whose full render takes about 4 s at a deep zoom, zoom and pause, then scroll: the wheel steps are ignored for about 4 s (one frame); expected the longest frame to be about an eighth of it. |
| `e32f8f2e` | c | M8: large images on the canvas draw from a smaller copy (#1483) (#1518) | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | crates/render/src/images.rs:16 and :161 decode and cache each image only at full size (Key has no side, there is no shown/DISPLAY_SIDES), and crates/render/src/paint.rs:143 draws canvas image fills from images::get, so two very large images shown zoomed out exceed the 512 MB cache and evict each other every frame. Reproduction: Two 16000x10000 images on one page at fit zoom, drag one over the other: each frame decodes about 640 MB again (upstream measured about 2.1 s a frame); expected the canvas to draw from 4096/8192 px copies (about 11 ms). |
| `f24a8312` | c | M8: the browser refines a zoom only once it has paused | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | Inline::poll (crates/ui-egui/src/render_worker.rs:282-292) runs the full render on the very next poll after a preview. In the browser build (apps/figcraft-web) every wheel step of a zoom therefore blocks on a full render. Identical to upstream's pre-fix code. Reproduction: In the web build, on a heavy page (full render about 3.7 s), zoom with 20 wheel steps: each step waits for a full render, about 141 s in total (upstream measured 10-12 s after the fix). |
| `adb1b430` | c | Speed up raster export: faster blur and spread passes | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | crates/render/src/fx.rs is byte-identical to upstream's pre-image (same md5): the row-by-row bounds-checked box_blur_h and O(r) morph that the commit replaces. Our render crate already depends on rayon. Reproduction: Exporting the examples' top-level frames as PNG/JPG/WebP at 2x takes about twice as long as upstream after the change (7.0 s against 3.6 s in its benchmark). |
| `0f85c229` | c | M9: avoid read-only snapshot rewrites and duplicate buffers | applies, still broken | M | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs | no | apps/figcraft-server/src/store.rs:1388-1393 compacts (a full snapshot rewrite) whenever the log is dirty, which issuing a client id makes it, so a viewer who only looks rewrites the snapshot on leaving; crates/collab/src/message.rs:718 encode builds the payload twice. Reproduction: A viewer joins and leaves an idle file without editing: compactions goes up by one (upstream: none). |
| `79d436a4` | c | perf(canvas): preview cold zoom and page transitions once they run long (M8) | applies, still broken | M | crates/ui-egui/src/canvas.rs | yes | Our Renders::wants_preview (crates/ui-egui/src/render_worker.rs:143) is the pre-fix one: it needs last.is_some() and a slow previous render, so the first render, a page or file switch, or a zoom after quick renders gets no preview. There is no watchdog thread and Done carries no seq. Reproduction: Open a 50k-layer page cold: the canvas shows the page colour until the full render is done; expected a coarse preview after about 12 ms. |
| `e37e0bc2` | c | perf(render): keep glass shapes a later render asks for again | applies, still broken | L | - | yes | crates/render/src/glass.rs (297 lines) works out a glass shape's coverage, distance transform and normals for every render; there is no ShapeCache or Fields cache. It's a ~200-line performance change. Reproduction: Drag a layer under a glass pane at 2400x1600: each frame recomputes the glass fields (upstream measured 49.5 vs 26.5 ms). |

Port ef4aecfd in this commit's form (a closed_in_group stash filled by file.close at crates/engine/src/cmd/file.rs:1361 and branches.rs:189) so the clone never arrives. |

| `e04c5d22` | d | text: use craft-font-catalog on Unix (#1765) | applies, still broken | M | - | yes | crates/text/src/fontdb.rs:723-728 system_font_dirs() on Linux/BSD still scans only /usr/share/fonts, /usr/local/share/fonts and the user folders, never $XDG_DATA_DIRS, /run/host/fonts or the folders fontconfig names. | `c668e217` | d | PDF: write with craft-libs' krilla, which bounds decoded PNGs (#1240) | applies, still broken | S | - | no | crates/pdf/src/draw.rs:461-467 hands a document's original PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP bytes to krilla 0.8.2 from crates.io, which decodes without an allocation cap; upstream patches krilla to craft-libs' copy (1 GiB cap, fallible reserve, no abort on a drawing panic). Mitigated: the bytes are first decoded by figcraft_render::images::get, whose limits bound the pixel size. The port adds a [patch.crates-io] git dependency on storytold/craft-libs. |

## Grouped commit list

Every upstream commit after `a0d1162` and up to `53259cf`, newest first, by group. "Files" is the number of files the commit touched, and "Areas" names the crates and folders. The full file list for each commit is in [`commits.tsv`](upstream-sync/commits.tsv). The features (e) are listed but not proposed for porting; Scrim has its own roadmap.

## Evidence files

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| [`upstream-sync/scan.py`](upstream-sync/scan.py) | Lists, groups and scores the commits. It needs the throwaway clone; the header says how to rebuild everything. |
| [`upstream-sync/overrides.tsv`](upstream-sync/overrides.tsv) | Hand corrections to the groups, and the audit fixes. |
| [`upstream-sync/commits.tsv`](upstream-sync/commits.tsv) | Every commit: hash, date, author, group, subject, files, scores, hint. |
| [`upstream-sync/verdicts.tsv`](upstream-sync/verdicts.tsv) | The applicability verdicts, with evidence and the reproductions described. |
| [`upstream-sync/tables.py`](upstream-sync/tables.py) | Builds this document's tables from the two TSV files. |
| [`upstream-sync/audit-map.tsv`](upstream-sync/audit-map.tsv) | Upstream's audit issues, their state and severity, and the commits that closed them. |
| [`upstream-sync/advisories.txt`](upstream-sync/advisories.txt) | The `cargo audit` and `cargo deny` output. |
| [`upstream-sync/repro-output.txt`](upstream-sync/repro-output.txt) | The five failing reproductions. The tests themselves are described above, not committed. |

## Ported from upstream (Fix AI stage 2a, 1 Nov 2026)

The security items from the audit above, ported by hand against Scrim's own code. Each commit names the upstream commits it ports (MIT OR Apache-2.0, as Scrim). A test was written first and failed on Scrim's code, except where the last column says why it couldn't.

| Item | Upstream commit(s) | Scrim commit | Test that failed first |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI-1 dependencies (rustls, rustls-webpki, webpki-roots, clipboard-win, tokio; `deny.toml`) | the versions `35da7b7c`, `8ae2e206` came with | `993a2f07` | `cargo audit` (9 vulnerabilities) and `cargo deny check advisories` (failed); after, both pass |
| Doc model: copying and the structure check move out of `doc/src/lib.rs` (no behaviour change) | – | `4ab8e6bc` | – |
| AI-2c ENG-3 (#1064): a stale id counter made copies overwrite layers | `8aa456b1`, `46b02d44` | `7f6bff96` | `doc` `copy_tests` (3) and `format` `stale_ids_tests` |
| AI-2b PLG-1 (#1055): a read-only plugin could upload the document | `b57c6778` | `ca3de58a` | `engine` `plugins_tests::a_plugin_never_uploads_the_document_or_reaches_a_server` (the plugin-call network guard's test is new API, so it couldn't run before) |
| AI-2d ENG-1 (#1056): document library paths stat'ed off this computer (UNC) | `b3e65c89` | `98b6800e` | `engine` `library_path_tests` cache test (the string-level path table is new API) |
| AI-2a COL-1 (#1054): anyone in a session could take over another's client id | `10702311` | `75e6d992` | The stage 1 reproduction (`upstream-sync/repro-output.txt`); `collab` `resume_tests` use the new field, so they couldn't compile before. Compatible both ways with upstream FigCraft 53259cf, checked against its own `collab` crate |
| AI-2e: a crafted progressive JPEG panicked the decoder | `9aac391e`, `0971d096` | `5eb8290d` | `format` `jpeg_tests` and `render` `jpeg_guard_tests` (the real zune-jpeg panic, from a file built in the test) |
| RND-1 (#1060): dash patterns too fine for their path | `494387a6`, `2baeff34` | `715adc65` | `pathops` `dash_limit_tests` (2.5M and 5M path elements) |
| FMT-2 (#1058) and ENG-2 (#1062): `validate`'s report; render-bounds recursion | `c857f8d0`, `4da6a11b` | `2662fed9` | `doc` `bounds_tests` (4,999 issues; a stack overflow abort) |
| FMT-1 (#1057): single scalar fields skipped the `.fig` value budget | `a80df8ed`, `1a454edd` | `d0f4de39` | `format` `fig::kiwi_budget_tests` |
| FMT-3 (#1059): `.fig` embedded images copied once per hash | `3009d19f` | `1996254c` | Couldn't fail first: the extra copies weren't observable from outside the import. `fig::embedded_copies_tests` tests the new function |
| COL-2 (#1063): a refused batch's fixes were quadratic | `08173cc5` | `664b587d` | `collab` `refused_fixes_tests` (45,150 creates for 300 layers) |
| RND-2 (#1061): nested isolated layers had no memory total | `96eed159` (the budget; its mask-area part, a performance change, isn't ported) | `c80162b9` | Couldn't fail first: it needs the budget's measuring hook, and the old behaviour shows only past 2 GiB of real allocation. `render` `layer_budget_tests` |
| AI-3: zip limits in every archive reader (Scrim's own) | – | `621606ef` | `format` `zip_bomb_tests` (7 of 8 failed: the importers read the whole 40 MiB bomb); `engine` `importers_tests` and the UI's en/es text test are new API |
| AI-4: the server installer and the Flatpak manifest | `cb796655` (its `curl --proto '=https'` part, for the installer) | `a31eb88a` | `xtask` `packaging_tests` (no network permission; `REPO` named upstream) |

**Deliberately unchanged (AI-5).** The local control channel (`apps/figcraft/src/control_server.rs`, upstream's open #1072) has no authentication still. It starts only with `--control` or `FIGCRAFT_CONTROL_PORT`, and the live checks depend on it.

**Live check.** [`upstream-sync/live.py`](upstream-sync/live.py) and [`upstream-sync/live-output.txt`](upstream-sync/live-output.txt): 13 of 13 checks passed, on scratch apps (HOME and settings, MCP on 7690 and 7691). They cover:
- a file edited, saved and reopened;
- `.scrim`, `.figcraft` and `.sketch` files opened;
- copy, paste and duplicate, also in a stale-counter file;
- an embedded PNG and a progressive JPEG reopened and drawn, checked by export;
- a crafted JPEG refused;
- a local live session hosted by one app and joined by a second, with edits both ways;
- no panics.

No window captures were taken: the windows drew no frames (macOS doesn't draw a window it hides), so `ui.screenshot` never answered.

## Ported from upstream (Fix AI stage 2b, 2 Nov 2026)

The bugs that lost or corrupted a person's work (port list 4.1 to 4.5), ported by hand against Scrim's own code. Each item also opens files the bug already damaged: they are repaired as they are read, and opening never writes the file. A test was written first and failed on Scrim's code unless the last column says otherwise.

| Item | Upstream commit(s) | Scrim commit | Test that failed first |
|---|---|---|---|
| LW-1 (4.1): page ruler guides lost on reopen, and the next save wouldn't open | `652bf35b` | `0fb59327` | `doc` `guides_tests` (2 of 4) and `format` `page_guides_tests` (both: the guide lost; "duplicate field `guides`") |
| Room in three frozen files (no behaviour change) | – | `d6d97d3b` | – |
| LW-2 (4.2): a NaN or infinite number saved as `null`, and the file wouldn't open | `8c08035b` (replaces `503b6d60`), `2d1fabda` | `5b3d5100` | `format` `null_numbers_tests` (2 of 3), `engine` `non_finite_tests` (2 of 3; the import guard's test is new API), `mcp` `tests/non_finite.rs` (both) |
| LW-5 (4.5): a crash during a version preview recovered the version, not the work | `019058ff` | `0e4526bb` | `figcraft` `recovery_tests::a_crash_during_a_version_preview_recovers_the_working_document` (1 layer recovered, not 2) |
| LW-3 (4.3): a save deleted the images past the budget | `c305e23a` (+ `03f4e6e0`, `df8edc6b`) | `2f3309ef` | `engine` `images_left_in_file_tests` (both: saving over the file kept 1 of 3 images); the UI notice's test is a new request |
| LW-4 (4.4): Save overwrote changes another app made to the file | `a648700f` + `0d7d9ebd` | `f86c623f` | `engine` `save_disk_changes_tests` (2 of 6: a plain save and auto-save wrote over the other app's file; the other 4 passed before too). The UI and MCP tests are of a new dialog and a new error, so they weren't run before |
| LW-4 follow-up: the refused save's raw message also showed as a toast behind the question | – | `0852c214` | `ui-egui` `lost_work_ui_tests` (the toast assertion, seen in the live check's capture) |

**Where Scrim goes further than upstream.**
- **LW-2:** files saved with `null` numbers open again, with each such value reset to its property's default, or to 0 where the layer must have a number; upstream only stopped writing them. Imports reset non-finite numbers too.
- **LW-3:** a save keeps the images it couldn't load by copying them from the file it last read or wrote; upstream refuses the save.
- **LW-4:** the window offers three choices instead of an error, and MCP returns a structured `fileChangedOnDisk` error with `force`.

**Live check.** [`upstream-sync/live-2b.py`](upstream-sync/live-2b.py) and [`upstream-sync/live-2b-output.txt`](upstream-sync/live-2b-output.txt): 10 of 10 checks passed on a scratch app (scratch HOME and settings, MCP on 7690, control 7979). Input went through the window's own control channel; port 7680 and the system pointer weren't used. The final run's capture, [`live-2b-save-conflict.png`](upstream-sync/live-2b-save-conflict.png), shows the "File changed on disk" question with its three choices and no raw error behind it, and LW-3's note from the step before ("5 that weren't loaded … were copied over"). Runs whose window drew no frames skipped the capture after one limited attempt.
